<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that minimum Vertex Cover is NP-hard to approximate within every fixed factor below two, even on simple unweighted graphs.

- **C1** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-gap)): For every fixed integer $m\geq4$, there is a deterministic polynomial-time reduction from $\mathrm{3SAT}$ to finite simple undirected unweighted graphs with the following guarantees: $$\begin{array}{ll} \text{the formula is satisfiable} &\Longrightarrow\quad \tau(G)<\left(\dfrac12+\dfrac1m\right)\|V(G)\|,\\[6pt] \text{the formula is unsatisfiable} &\Longrightarrow\quad \tau(G)>\left(1-\dfrac1m\right)\|V(G)\|. \end{array}$$ The output lists all vertices and edges explicitly.
  - Explanation: Produces explicit graphs separating satisfiable formulas from unsatisfiable formulas by minimum cover size.
- **C2** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-hardness)): For every fixed real $\alpha$ with $1\leq\alpha<2$, approximating Vertex Cover within factor $\alpha$ is NP-hard. Thus, unless $\mathrm P=\mathrm{NP}$, no deterministic polynomial-time algorithm achieves factor $2-\varepsilon$ for any fixed $\varepsilon\in(0,1]$.
  - Explanation: Rules out every fixed improvement over the maximal-matching approximation in deterministic polynomial time unless the stated complexity classes coincide.
- **C3** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-src-hardness)): For every fixed rational $\sigma\in(0,1)$, there are nonempty constant alphabets and a deterministic polynomial-time reduction from $\mathrm{3SAT}$ to instances of the form above such that satisfiable formulas give $\mathop{\mathrm{val}}(\Phi)=1$, and unsatisfiable formulas give $\mathop{\mathrm{val}}(\Phi)\leq\sigma$.
  - Explanation: Supplies the external perfect-completeness source promise with arbitrarily small fixed positive soundness.
- **C4** (theorem, [source](#stmt-graph-norm)): The family $\mathcal I$ is hereditary and contains every coordinate singleton. Formula <a href="#graph:norm-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="graph:norm-definition">[graph:norm-definition]</a> defines a norm. For each fixed query $i$, the increment is linear in $s$ and obeys $$\label{graph:increment} \left\\|z(i,s)\right\\|_*\leq\\|s\\|_\infty.$$
  - Explanation: Uses hereditary compatible selections to obtain a norm and a bound on each query increment.
- **C5** (theorem, [source](#stmt-graph-local-norm)): Let $\mathcal B\subseteq\mathcal Q$ be a set of query blocks supporting $z\in\mathbb R^{\mathcal P}$. Then $$\label{graph:local-formula} \left\\|z\right\\|_*=\max_{\substack{I\in\mathcal I:\\ (i,\lambda)\in I\ \Longrightarrow\ i\in\mathcal B}} \left\|\sum_{(i,\lambda)\in I}z_{i,\lambda}\right\|.$$ For $z=S_v+S_w$, this maximum can be evaluated by at most $(1+r)^{2d}$ choices, each checked by a scan of the source constraints.
  - Explanation: Restricts norm evaluation to supported query blocks and checks compatibility by scanning source constraints.
- **C6** (construction, [source](#stmt-graph-polynomial)): For a fixed integer $m\geq4$, a fixed even square $d$ satisfying [src:dimension-bounds](#src:dimension-bounds), and fixed source alphabets, the graph $G$ can be output explicitly in deterministic time polynomial in the source bit length. Every edge test is exact.
  - Explanation: Enumerates the graph deterministically with exact integer edge tests when all construction parameters and alphabets are fixed.
- **C7** (bound, [source](#stmt-graph-anticoncentration)): If $T_1,\ldots,T_d$ are independent uniform elements of $\mathcal T$, then $$\label{graph:tail} \Pr\left[\sum_{j=1}^dT_j>t\right] >\frac12-\sqrt2\left(a+\frac1{\sqrt d}\right) >\frac12-\frac1m.$$
  - Explanation: Bounds the positive tail of independent symmetric grid weights to support completeness.
- **C8** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-graph-completeness)): If $\mathop{\mathrm{val}}(\Phi)=1$, then $G$ has an independent set of size greater than $(1/2-1/m)n$. Consequently, $$\tau(G)<\left(\frac12+\frac1m\right)n.$$
  - Explanation: Uses a satisfying source labeling to select a large independent set and hence a small complementary cover.
- **C9** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ana-function)): Suppose an independent set contains at least $b_0n$ vertices with $\left\\|S_v\right\\|_*>t$. There is a fixed nonempty finite set $A_*\subset\mathbb R^{\mathcal P}$ such that $$\Pr_{v\in V(G)}[S_v\in A_*]\geq b_0, \qquad \left\\|x+y\right\\|_*>2t\quad(x,y\in A_*),$$ where the vertex is uniform. For a nonempty finite set $B$, write $\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_*(z,B)=\min_{b\in B}\left\\|z-b\right\\|_*$. The function $$F(z)=\frac12\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_*(z,-A_*)-\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_*(z,A_*)\bigr) \label{ana:distance-function}$$ is odd and $1$-Lipschitz for $\left\\|\cdot\right\\|_*$, and $F(z)>t$ on $A_*$.* *Let $\mathcal E=\binom{[d]}2$. Draw independent uniform occurrence seeds $\boldsymbol c=(c_e)_{e\in\mathcal E}\in[M]^{\mathcal E}$ and independent weight blocks $s_j$ uniform on $[-1,1]^r$, independently of the seeds. Put $$f(\boldsymbol c,\boldsymbol s) =F\left(\sum_{j=1}^d z(i_j(\boldsymbol c),s_j)\right). \label{ana:f}$$ This bounded function satisfies $$\mathbb E_{\boldsymbol s}f(\boldsymbol c,\boldsymbol s)=0 \quad\hbox{for every }\boldsymbol c, \qquad \mathbb Ef^2\geq\nu d. \label{ana:variance}$$ On the weight cubes it has the following three oscillation bounds:* 1. *Replacing one weight block $s_j$, with all other data fixed, changes $f$ by at most $2$.* 2. *Replacing one seed $c_e$, with all other data fixed, changes $f$ by at most $4$.* 3. *Fix $k\in[d]$. Simultaneously replacing any seeds incident to $k$, while keeping all nonincident seeds, all weights, and the question received at every opposite position $j\ne k$ from $\{j,k\}$ unchanged, changes $f$ by at most $2$.* *The set $A_*$, the function $F$, and its formula are fixed from an independent set of the entire graph before the fresh seeds above are drawn. A seed enters $f$ only through its endpoint questions.
  - Explanation: Builds one fixed odd Lipschitz function from separated high-norm vectors and controls variance and input oscillations.
- **C10** (theorem, [source](#stmt-res-private-variance)): Let $\nu>0$ and let $h$ be an integer with $2\leq h\leq d$. Suppose the bounded Borel measurable function $f$ on $\Omega$ satisfies $$\label{res:variance-hypotheses} \mathbb E_{\mathbf s}f(\mathbf c,\mathbf s)=0 \quad\text{for every }\mathbf c, \qquad \mathbb Ef^2\geq\nu d.$$ Suppose also that the following oscillation bounds hold with all coordinates not mentioned kept fixed:* 1. *Changing one weight block $s_j$ changes $f$ by at most $2$.* 2. *Changing one seed $c_e$ changes $f$ by at most $4$.* 3. *For any $k\in[d]$, simultaneously changing seeds incident to $k$, while preserving $Q_{\{k,j\},j}(c_{\{k,j\}})$ for every $j\ne k$, changes $f$ by at most $2$.* *If $$\label{res:scale} 16h+32h^2\leq\frac{\nu d}{2},$$ then there are a fixed $h$-element set $J$ and fixed frozen data $\mathbf a$ such that $$\label{res:variance} \sum_{j\in J}\mathbb E_{u_j}\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}_{s_j}H_j(u_j,s_j) \geq\frac{\nu h}{4}.$$ The law in <a href="#res:variance" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="res:variance">[res:variance]</a> is the product law of the remaining hidden seeds and batch weights described above. In particular it is not conditioned on an event involving a draw of those remaining variables.
  - Explanation: Uses orthogonal decompositions and star resampling to retain own-question variance after freezing background data.
- **C11** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ana-gradient)): There is a deterministic measurable rule $g=(g_j)_{j\in J}\in\mathbb R^{J\times[r]}$, defined at every batch query and real-weight tuple, that equals $\nabla\psi_{\boldsymbol i}$ outside a finite union of proper affine hyperplanes for each fixed query tuple and fixed frozen data. At every tuple, $$g\in\mathop{\mathrm{conv}}\{\pm v_I(\boldsymbol i):I\in\mathcal I\}. \label{ana:hull}$$ Consequently, $$\\|g_j\\|_1\leq1, \qquad g_{j,t'}=0\quad\hbox{if }t'\notin \operatorname{slot}_{i_j}(\Lambda_{i_j}). \label{ana:block-bound}$$ The rule uses the fixed formula for $F$, the frozen increments, and the supplied batch queries and weights. It does not inspect which hidden seed occurrences produced those queries.
  - Explanation: Constructs a measurable gradient rule in the hull of signed compatible selections, including exceptional weights.
- **C12** (bound, [source](#stmt-ana-cube)): For each $j\in J$ and supported own tuple $u$, the function $H_j(u,\cdot)$ is $1$-Lipschitz with respect to $\\|\cdot\\|_\infty$. For almost every own $s\in[-1,1]^r$, $$\partial_{s^{(t')}}H_j(u,s)=\bar g_{j,t'}(u,s), \qquad t'\in[r]. \label{ana:conditional-derivative}$$ Its variance satisfies the dimension-independent bound $$\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}_s H_j(u,s) \leq2\mathbb E_s\\|\bar g_j(u,s)\\|_2^2. \label{ana:cube-variance}$$ Consequently, under the fixed restricted law, $$\sum_{j\in J}\mathbb E\\|\bar g_j(u_j,s_j)\\|_2^2 \geq\nu h/8=\beta h. \label{ana:energy}$$
  - Explanation: Identifies conditional derivatives almost everywhere and bounds cube variance without an alphabet-size factor.
- **C13** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ana-lists)): The functions $\mathcal L_j$ are deterministic total measurable maps returning at most $\ell$ valid labels. Once $\Phi,F,J,\boldsymbol a$ and the conditional kernels above are fixed, each list uses only its own questions $u_j$ and weights $s_j$. In particular it has no additional access to the actual hidden seed occurrences or to any other actual batch input. Under the fixed restricted product law, $$\mathbb EZ\geq\frac{\beta h}{2}=2. \label{ana:list-hits}$$
  - Explanation: Thresholds own-question conditional gradients into bounded valid lists and transfers their mass to compatible hits.
- **C14** (bound, [source](#stmt-dec-pair)): For every fixed $J,\mathbf a$ and fixed list maps satisfying [dec:local_lists](#dec:local_lists), $$\Pr(E_{jk})\leq\ell^2\sigma \quad(j,k\in J,\ j<k), \qquad \mathbb EZ\leq1+\binom h2\ell^2\sigma =\frac32-\frac1{2h}<\frac32. \label{dec:pair_bound}$$
  - Explanation: Decodes fixed ranks into source labelings using only own endpoints and bounds compatible pair events.
- **C15** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-dec-soundness)): For the graph constructed in [graph:section](#graph:section), assume <a href="#src:restriction-budget" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="src:restriction-budget">[src:restriction-budget]</a>. If $\mathop{\mathrm{val}}(\Phi)\leq\sigma$, every independent set has size strictly less than $n/m$. Consequently $\tau(G)>(1-1/m)n$.
  - Explanation: Contradicts the lower and upper expected-hit bounds to exclude large independent sets under source soundness.

          "exact_quote": "For every fixed integer $m\\geq4$, there is a deterministic polynomial-time reduction from $\\mathrm{3SAT}$ to finite simple undirected unweighted graphs with the following guarantees: $$\\begin{array}{ll}\n\\text{the formula is satisfiable}\n &\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n \\tau(G)<\\left(\\dfrac12+\\dfrac1m\\right)|V(G)|,\\\\[6pt]\n\\text{the formula is unsatisfiable}\n &\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n \\tau(G)>\\left(1-\\dfrac1m\\right)|V(G)|.\n\\end{array}$$ The output lists all vertices and edges explicitly.",
          "source_id": "PM-102.3"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ana-function",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose an independent set contains at least $b_0n$ vertices with $\\left\\|S_v\\right\\|_*>t$. There is a fixed nonempty finite set $A_*\\subset\\mathbb R^{\\mathcal P}$ such that $$\\Pr_{v\\in V(G)}[S_v\\in A_*]\\geq b_0,\n \\qquad\n \\left\\|x+y\\right\\|_*>2t\\quad(x,y\\in A_*),$$ where the vertex is uniform. For a nonempty finite set $B$, write $\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}_*(z,B)=\\min_{b\\in B}\\left\\|z-b\\right\\|_*$. The function $$F(z)=\\frac12\\bigl(\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}_*(z,-A_*)-\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}_*(z,A_*)\\bigr)\n \\label{ana:distance-function}$$ is odd and $1$-Lipschitz for $\\left\\|\\cdot\\right\\|_*$, and $F(z)>t$ on $A_*$.*\n\n\n*Let $\\mathcal E=\\binom{[d]}2$. Draw independent uniform occurrence seeds $\\boldsymbol c=(c_e)_{e\\in\\mathcal E}\\in[M]^{\\mathcal E}$ and independent weight blocks $s_j$ uniform on $[-1,1]^r$, independently of the seeds. Put $$f(\\boldsymbol c,\\boldsymbol s)\n    =F\\left(\\sum_{j=1}^d z(i_j(\\boldsymbol c),s_j)\\right).\n \\label{ana:f}$$ This bounded function satisfies $$\\mathbb E_{\\boldsymbol s}f(\\boldsymbol c,\\boldsymbol s)=0\n       \\quad\\hbox{for every }\\boldsymbol c,\n \\qquad\n \\mathbb Ef^2\\geq\\nu d.\n \\label{ana:variance}$$ On the weight cubes it has the following three oscillation bounds:*\n\n1.  *Replacing one weight block $s_j$, with all other data fixed, changes $f$ by at most $2$.*\n\n2.  *Replacing one seed $c_e$, with all other data fixed, changes $f$ by at most $4$.*\n\n3.  *Fix $k\\in[d]$. Simultaneously replacing any seeds incident to $k$, while keeping all nonincident seeds, all weights, and the question received at every opposite position $j\\ne k$ from $\\{j,k\\}$ unchanged, changes $f$ by at most $2$.*\n\n*The set $A_*$, the function $F$, and its formula are fixed from an independent set of the entire graph before the fresh seeds above are drawn. A seed enters $f$ only through its endpoint questions.",
        "source_id": "PM-102.3"
      },

          "anchor": "stmt-ana-function",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose an independent set contains at least $b_0n$ vertices with $\\left\\|S_v\\right\\|_*>t$. There is a fixed nonempty finite set $A_*\\subset\\mathbb R^{\\mathcal P}$ such that $$\\Pr_{v\\in V(G)}[S_v\\in A_*]\\geq b_0,\n \\qquad\n \\left\\|x+y\\right\\|_*>2t\\quad(x,y\\in A_*),$$ where the vertex is uniform. For a nonempty finite set $B$, write $\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}_*(z,B)=\\min_{b\\in B}\\left\\|z-b\\right\\|_*$. The function $$F(z)=\\frac12\\bigl(\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}_*(z,-A_*)-\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}_*(z,A_*)\\bigr)\n \\label{ana:distance-function}$$ is odd and $1$-Lipschitz for $\\left\\|\\cdot\\right\\|_*$, and $F(z)>t$ on $A_*$.*\n\n\n*Let $\\mathcal E=\\binom{[d]}2$. Draw independent uniform occurrence seeds $\\boldsymbol c=(c_e)_{e\\in\\mathcal E}\\in[M]^{\\mathcal E}$ and independent weight blocks $s_j$ uniform on $[-1,1]^r$, independently of the seeds. Put $$f(\\boldsymbol c,\\boldsymbol s)\n    =F\\left(\\sum_{j=1}^d z(i_j(\\boldsymbol c),s_j)\\right).\n \\label{ana:f}$$ This bounded function satisfies $$\\mathbb E_{\\boldsymbol s}f(\\boldsymbol c,\\boldsymbol s)=0\n       \\quad\\hbox{for every }\\boldsymbol c,\n \\qquad\n \\mathbb Ef^2\\geq\\nu d.\n \\label{ana:variance}$$ On the weight cubes it has the following three oscillation bounds:*\n\n1.  *Replacing one weight block $s_j$, with all other data fixed, changes $f$ by at most $2$.*\n\n2.  *Replacing one seed $c_e$, with all other data fixed, changes $f$ by at most $4$.*\n\n3.  *Fix $k\\in[d]$. Simultaneously replacing any seeds incident to $k$, while keeping all nonincident seeds, all weights, and the question received at every opposite position $j\\ne k$ from $\\{j,k\\}$ unchanged, changes $f$ by at most $2$.*\n\n*The set $A_*$, the function $F$, and its formula are fixed from an independent set of the entire graph before the fresh seeds above are drawn. A seed enters $f$ only through its endpoint questions.",
          "source_id": "PM-102.3"
        }
      ],

The function $$F(z)=\\frac12\\bigl(\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}_*(z,-A_*)-\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}_*(z,A_*)\\bigr)\n \\label{ana:distance-function}$$ is odd and $1$-Lipschitz for $\\left\\|\\cdot\\right\\|_*$, and $F(z)>t$ on $A_*$.*\n\n\n*Let $\\mathcal E=\\binom{[d]}2$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-res-private-variance",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\nu>0$ and let $h$ be an integer with $2\\leq h\\leq d$. Suppose the bounded Borel measurable function $f$ on $\\Omega$ satisfies $$\\label{res:variance-hypotheses}\n \\mathbb E_{\\mathbf s}f(\\mathbf c,\\mathbf s)=0\n       \\quad\\text{for every }\\mathbf c,\n \\qquad \\mathbb Ef^2\\geq\\nu d.$$ Suppose also that the following oscillation bounds hold with all coordinates not mentioned kept fixed:*\n\n1.  *Changing one weight block $s_j$ changes $f$ by at most $2$.*\n\n2.  *Changing one seed $c_e$ changes $f$ by at most $4$.*\n\n3.  *For any $k\\in[d]$, simultaneously changing seeds incident to $k$, while preserving $Q_{\\{k,j\\},j}(c_{\\{k,j\\}})$ for every $j\\ne k$, changes $f$ by at most $2$.*\n\n\n\n*If $$\\label{res:scale}\n 16h+32h^2\\leq\\frac{\\nu d}{2},$$ then there are a fixed $h$-element set $J$ and fixed frozen data $\\mathbf a$ such that $$\\label{res:variance}\n \\sum_{j\\in J}\\mathbb E_{u_j}\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}_{s_j}H_j(u_j,s_j)\n       \\geq\\frac{\\nu h}{4}.$$ The law in <a href=\"#res:variance\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"res:variance\">[res:variance]</a> is the product law of the remaining hidden seeds and batch weights described above. In particular it is not conditioned on an event involving a draw of those remaining variables.",
        "source_id": "PM-102.3"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ana-gradient",
        "exact_quote": "There is a deterministic measurable rule $g=(g_j)_{j\\in J}\\in\\mathbb R^{J\\times[r]}$, defined at every batch query and real-weight tuple, that equals $\\nabla\\psi_{\\boldsymbol i}$ outside a finite union of proper affine hyperplanes for each fixed query tuple and fixed frozen data. At every tuple, $$g\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{conv}}\\{\\pm v_I(\\boldsymbol i):I\\in\\mathcal I\\}.\n \\label{ana:hull}$$ Consequently, $$\\|g_j\\|_1\\leq1,\n \\qquad\n g_{j,t'}=0\\quad\\hbox{if }t'\\notin\n                   \\operatorname{slot}_{i_j}(\\Lambda_{i_j}).\n \\label{ana:block-bound}$$ The rule uses the fixed formula for $F$, the frozen increments, and the supplied batch queries and weights. It does not inspect which hidden seed occurrences produced those queries.",
        "source_id": "PM-102.3"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ana-cube",
        "exact_quote": "For each $j\\in J$ and supported own tuple $u$, the function $H_j(u,\\cdot)$ is $1$-Lipschitz with respect to $\\|\\cdot\\|_\\infty$. For almost every own $s\\in[-1,1]^r$, $$\\partial_{s^{(t')}}H_j(u,s)=\\bar g_{j,t'}(u,s),\n \\qquad t'\\in[r].\n \\label{ana:conditional-derivative}$$ Its variance satisfies the dimension-independent bound $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}_s H_j(u,s)\n       \\leq2\\mathbb E_s\\|\\bar g_j(u,s)\\|_2^2.\n \\label{ana:cube-variance}$$ Consequently, under the fixed restricted law, $$\\sum_{j\\in J}\\mathbb E\\|\\bar g_j(u_j,s_j)\\|_2^2\n      \\geq\\nu h/8=\\beta h.\n \\label{ana:energy}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-102.3"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ana-lists",
        "exact_quote": "The functions $\\mathcal L_j$ are deterministic total measurable maps returning at most $\\ell$ valid labels. Once $\\Phi,F,J,\\boldsymbol a$ and the conditional kernels above are fixed, each list uses only its own questions $u_j$ and weights $s_j$. In particular it has no additional access to the actual hidden seed occurrences or to any other actual batch input. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Vertex cover** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A vertex cover of a graph is a set of vertices meeting every edge.
- **Independent set** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Its complement is an independent set, a set containing no two adjacent vertices.
- **Approximation guarantee** ([source](#sec:introduction)): An $\alpha$-approximation returns an actual cover of size at most $\alpha\tau(G)$.
- **Label Cover instance** ([source](#sec:source)): A *Label Cover instance* $\Phi$ consists of two disjoint finite variable sets $U,V$, two nonempty finite alphabets $\Sigma_U,\Sigma_V$, and an explicit nonempty list of $M$ constraint occurrences.
- **Labeling** ([source](#sec:source)): A labeling $A$ assigns each variable a symbol from its side’s alphabet.
- **Source value** ([source](#sec:source)): The value $\mathop{\mathrm{val}}(\Phi)$ is the maximum fraction of satisfied occurrences.
- **Compatible selection** ([source](#pm-h-12)): A *compatible selection* is a subset $I\subseteq\mathcal P$ with at most one label per query, such that the selected labels agree on overlaps and their induced labeling satisfies every source constraint whose two endpoints lie in $$P(I)=\bigcup_{(i,\lambda)\in I}P(i).$$
- **Compatibility norm** ([source](#graph:norm-definition)): For $z\in\mathbb R^{\mathcal P}$, define $$\label{graph:norm-definition} \left\\|z\right\\|_*=\max_{I\in\mathcal I} \left\|\sum_{(i,\lambda)\in I}z_{i,\lambda}\right\|.$$

|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | a | = | $\frac1{4m}$ | Fixed construction parameters chosen before requesting the source alphabets. |
| Q2 | b_0 | = | $\frac1{2m}$ | Fixed construction parameters chosen before requesting the source alphabets. |
| Q3 | \nu | = | $\frac1{2^7m^3}$ | Fixed construction parameters chosen before requesting the source alphabets. |
| Q4 | \beta | = | $\frac1{2^{10}m^3}$ | Fixed construction parameters chosen before requesting the source alphabets. |
| Q5 | \ell | = | $2^{11}m^3$ | Fixed construction parameters chosen before requesting the source alphabets. |
| Q6 | h | = | $2^{12}m^3$ | Fixed construction parameters chosen before requesting the source alphabets. |
| Q7 | d | = | $2^{38}m^{10}$ | Fixed construction parameters chosen before requesting the source alphabets. |
| Q8 | \sigma | = | $\frac1{2^{46}m^{12}}$ | Fixed construction parameters chosen before requesting the source alphabets. |
| Q9 | p | = | $2d+1$ | Fixed construction parameters chosen before requesting the source alphabets. |
| Q10 | t | = | $2^{17}m^4$ | Fixed construction parameters chosen before requesting the source alphabets. |
| Q11 | Gap ratio | = | $2-\frac6{m+2}$ | Fixed construction parameters chosen before requesting the source alphabets. |
| Q12 | Retained own-question variance | ≥ | $\frac{\nu h}{4}$ | Bounded Borel functions with the stated zero-mean, variance, oscillation and restriction-scale hypotheses. |
| Q13 | Conditional cube variance | ≤ | $2\mathbb E_s\\|\bar g_j(u,s)\\|_2^2$ | Supported own tuples under the fixed restricted product law; derivatives are identified almost everywhere. |
| Q14 | Expected compatible hits | ≥ | $2$ | Fixed source instance, fixed odd Lipschitz function, fixed restriction and own-input list maps from conditional gradients. |
| Q15 | Expected compatible hits | < | $\frac32$ | Fixed own-input list maps with bounded list size, independent hidden occurrences and source value at most the selected soundness. |
| Q16 | Completeness cover size | < | $\left(\frac12+\frac1m\right)n$ | Source value equals one and the graph satisfies the construction conditions. |
| Q17 | Soundness cover size | > | $(1-1/m)n$ | Source value is at most the chosen soundness and the restriction-budget conditions hold. |

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-res-private-variance",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\nu>0$ and let $h$ be an integer with $2\\leq h\\leq d$. Suppose the bounded Borel measurable function $f$ on $\\Omega$ satisfies $$\\label{res:variance-hypotheses}\n \\mathbb E_{\\mathbf s}f(\\mathbf c,\\mathbf s)=0\n       \\quad\\text{for every }\\mathbf c,\n \\qquad \\mathbb Ef^2\\geq\\nu d.$$ Suppose also that the following oscillation bounds hold with all coordinates not mentioned kept fixed:*\n\n1.  *Changing one weight block $s_j$ changes $f$ by at most $2$.*\n\n2.  *Changing one seed $c_e$ changes $f$ by at most $4$.*\n\n3.  *For any $k\\in[d]$, simultaneously changing seeds incident to $k$, while preserving $Q_{\\{k,j\\},j}(c_{\\{k,j\\}})$ for every $j\\ne k$, changes $f$ by at most $2$.*\n\n\n\n*If $$\\label{res:scale}\n 16h+32h^2\\leq\\frac{\\nu d}{2},$$ then there are a fixed $h$-element set $J$ and fixed frozen data $\\mathbf a$ such that $$\\label{res:variance}\n \\sum_{j\\in J}\\mathbb E_{u_j}\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}_{s_j}H_j(u_j,s_j)\n       \\geq\\frac{\\nu h}{4}.$$ The law in <a href=\"#res:variance\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"res:variance\">[res:variance]</a> is the product law of the remaining hidden seeds and batch weights described above. In particular it is not conditioned on an event involving a draw of those remaining variables.",
          "source_id": "PM-102.3"
        }
      ],

          "anchor": "stmt-ana-cube",
          "exact_quote": "For each $j\\in J$ and supported own tuple $u$, the function $H_j(u,\\cdot)$ is $1$-Lipschitz with respect to $\\|\\cdot\\|_\\infty$. For almost every own $s\\in[-1,1]^r$, $$\\partial_{s^{(t')}}H_j(u,s)=\\bar g_{j,t'}(u,s),\n \\qquad t'\\in[r].\n \\label{ana:conditional-derivative}$$ Its variance satisfies the dimension-independent bound $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}_s H_j(u,s)\n       \\leq2\\mathbb E_s\\|\\bar g_j(u,s)\\|_2^2.\n \\label{ana:cube-variance}$$ Consequently, under the fixed restricted law, $$\\sum_{j\\in J}\\mathbb E\\|\\bar g_j(u_j,s_j)\\|_2^2\n      \\geq\\nu h/8=\\beta h.\n \\label{ana:energy}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-102.3"
        }

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ana-lists",
          "exact_quote": "The functions $\\mathcal L_j$ are deterministic total measurable maps returning at most $\\ell$ valid labels. Once $\\Phi,F,J,\\boldsymbol a$ and the conditional kernels above are fixed, each list uses only its own questions $u_j$ and weights $s_j$. In particular it has no additional access to the actual hidden seed occurrences or to any other actual batch input. <a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-src-hardness) / [proof](#sec:source): Supplies the external perfect-completeness source promise with arbitrarily small fixed positive soundness. -> Supplies the external perfect-completeness source promise with arbitrarily small fixed positive soundness.
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-graph-norm) / [proof](#proof-stmt-graph-norm): Uses hereditary compatible selections to obtain a norm and a bound on each query increment. -> Uses hereditary compatible selections to obtain a norm and a bound on each query increment.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-graph-local-norm) / [proof](#proof-stmt-graph-local-norm): Restricts norm evaluation to supported query blocks and checks compatibility by scanning source constraints. -> Restricts norm evaluation to supported query blocks and checks compatibility by scanning source constraints.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-graph-polynomial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-graph-polynomial): Enumerates the graph deterministically with exact integer edge tests when all construction parameters and alphabets are fixed. -> Enumerates the graph deterministically with exact integer edge tests when all construction parameters and alphabets are fixed.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-graph-anticoncentration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-graph-anticoncentration): Bounds the positive tail of independent symmetric grid weights to support completeness. -> Bounds the positive tail of independent symmetric grid weights to support completeness.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-graph-completeness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-graph-completeness): Uses a satisfying source labeling to select a large independent set and hence a small complementary cover. -> Uses a satisfying source labeling to select a large independent set and hence a small complementary cover.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-ana-function) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ana-function): Builds one fixed odd Lipschitz function from separated high-norm vectors and controls variance and input oscillations. -> Builds one fixed odd Lipschitz function from separated high-norm vectors and controls variance and input oscillations.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-res-private-variance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-res-private-variance): Uses orthogonal decompositions and star resampling to retain own-question variance after freezing background data. -> Uses orthogonal decompositions and star resampling to retain own-question variance after freezing background data.
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-ana-gradient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ana-gradient): Constructs a measurable gradient rule in the hull of signed compatible selections, including exceptional weights. -> Constructs a measurable gradient rule in the hull of signed compatible selections, including exceptional weights.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-ana-cube) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ana-cube): Identifies conditional derivatives almost everywhere and bounds cube variance without an alphabet-size factor. -> Identifies conditional derivatives almost everywhere and bounds cube variance without an alphabet-size factor.
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-ana-lists) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ana-lists): Thresholds own-question conditional gradients into bounded valid lists and transfers their mass to compatible hits. -> Thresholds own-question conditional gradients into bounded valid lists and transfers their mass to compatible hits.
- **R12** [claim](#stmt-dec-pair) / [proof](#proof-stmt-dec-pair): Decodes fixed ranks into source labelings using only own endpoints and bounds compatible pair events. -> Decodes fixed ranks into source labelings using only own endpoints and bounds compatible pair events.
- **R13** [claim](#stmt-dec-soundness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-dec-soundness): Contradicts the lower and upper expected-hit bounds to exclude large independent sets under source soundness. -> Contradicts the lower and upper expected-hit bounds to exclude large independent sets under source soundness.
- **R14** [claim](#stmt-thm-gap) / [proof](#sec:conclusion): Produces explicit graphs separating satisfiable formulas from unsatisfiable formulas by minimum cover size. -> Produces explicit graphs separating satisfiable formulas from unsatisfiable formulas by minimum cover size.
- **R15** [claim](#stmt-thm-hardness) / [proof](#sec:conclusion): Rules out every fixed improvement over the maximal-matching approximation in deterministic polynomial time unless the stated complexity classes coincide. -> Rules out every fixed improvement over the maximal-matching approximation in deterministic polynomial time unless the stated complexity classes coincide.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **local-norm**: The compatibility norm can be evaluated by finite selection enumeration and exact source-constraint checks.
- **fixed-parameters**: Polynomial construction time holds with the gap parameter, dimension, and source alphabets fixed.
- **own-inputs**: Each decoding list must depend only on its own endpoint questions and weights.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **overlap-only**: Check label agreement and omit constraints between selected scopes. -> An agreeing union can violate a source constraint.
- **leaked-inputs**: Give a decoding list the opposite endpoint or occurrence index. -> The decoded labeling can depend on the sampled occurrence.
- **uniform-runtime**: Treat the reduction as efficient with a varying desired approximation factor. -> The polynomial degree and constants depend on fixed construction parameters.
- **hardness-certified**: Use finite norm tests as certification of the approximation hardness theorem. -> Finite enumeration does not establish the restriction and decoding arguments.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The local norm evaluation is a finite constructive result. The code tests compatible selection enumeration; the analytic hardness proof is outside its scope.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON array of binary projection instances with scopes and rational weights.
# PRECONDITIONS: At most 4 variables, 3 blocks, 8 constraints; binary alphabets; ordered unique scopes.
# OUTPUTS: JSON list of exact compatibility norms as canonical rational strings.
# TIME: Exponential in the total scope size and block count; input sizes are capped.
# SPACE: Exponential local-label storage; bounded enumeration workspace.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact FLINT rational arithmetic, no floating-point comparisons.
# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception and exits nonzero.
import sys,json,itertools
from flint import fmpq

def norm(c):
    assert 0 <= c['n'] <= 4 and len(c['blocks']) <= 3 and len(c['constraints']) <= 8
    assert len(c['weights']) == len(c['blocks'])
    for x,y,p in c['constraints']:
        assert 0 <= x < c['n'] and 0 <= y < c['n'] and x != y
        assert len(p)==2 and all(v in (0,1) for v in p)
    choices=[]
    for scope,ws in zip(c['blocks'],c['weights']):
        assert len(scope)==len(set(scope)) and all(0<=v<c['n'] for v in scope)
        assert len(ws)==2**len(scope)
        row=[({},fmpq(0))]
        for idx,bits in enumerate(itertools.product((0,1),repeat=len(scope))):
            a=dict(zip(scope,bits))
            if all(x not in a or y not in a or p[a[x]]==a[y] for x,y,p in c['constraints']):
                row.append((a,fmpq(ws[idx])))
        choices.append(row)
    best=fmpq(0)
    for pick in itertools.product(*choices):
        merged={}; total=fmpq(0); good=True
        for a,w in pick:
            if any(v in merged and merged[v]!=b for v,b in a.items()):
                good=False; break
            merged.update(a)
            total += w
        if not good: continue
        if any(x in merged and y in merged and p[merged[x]]!=merged[y] for x,y,p in c['constraints']): continue
        best=max(best,abs(total))
    return str(best)
print(json.dumps([norm(c) for c in json.load(sys.stdin)]))
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A concrete software deployment contract is ABSENT. The paper supplies an explicit graph reduction, but does not specify a library interface or deployed service. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A simulation contract is ABSENT. Label Cover is a constraint system here, and random variables describe the analysis of an explicitly enumerated graph. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A tool interface and integration contract are ABSENT. Exact construction is stated, but no executable tool specification is supplied. |
| algorithms | True | strong | implementable | inferred | The paper specifies deterministic explicit graph construction and exact local edge tests for fixed construction parameters and source alphabets. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The finite query-block and slot representation supports exact local norm evaluation. It is a representation contract rather than a general data-structure performance result. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A hardware contract is ABSENT. The construction is specified in deterministic bit complexity with exact edge tests, without an accelerator model. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A cryptographic contract is ABSENT. The stated result is approximation NP-hardness, without an average-case distribution or a security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A learning contract is ABSENT. The paper studies approximation guarantees for Vertex Cover and uses randomness to analyse an explicit reduction. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The result supplies a worst-case boundary for deterministic polynomial-time Vertex Cover approximation on finite simple undirected unweighted graphs. It also retains the maximal-matching factor-$2$ upper bound. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A distributed contract is ABSENT. The paper gives explicit deterministic graph output without a communication, fault, or consistency model. |
| other (computational_complexity) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | The result is directly relevant to computational complexity: an explicit gap reduction yields fixed-factor approximation hardness. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-1** (hypothesis): The exclusion of deterministic polynomial-time algorithms with a fixed improvement below the stated approximation threshold is conditional on the complexity hypothesis. Blocked use: ['Do not present the algorithmic exclusion as an unconditional separation of complexity classes.']
- **limit-2** (counterexample_scope): The gap reduction outputs finite simple undirected unweighted graphs. A separate guarantee for other graph models is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Do not transfer the stated input contract directly to weighted or directed cover instances.']
- **limit-3** (approximation): Hardness concerns a fixed approximation factor below $2$; the maximal-matching endpoint construction still supplies the stated factor-$2$ cover. Blocked use: ['Do not exclude the endpoint approximation or improvements whose saving tends to zero with graph order.']
- **limit-4** (hypothesis): Polynomial construction fixes the gap parameter, dimension and source alphabets. Its constants and degree can depend on those choices. Blocked use: ['Do not claim a uniform practical running-time guarantee as the requested approximation factor varies.']
- **limit-5** (nonconstructive): The independent set, distance function and conditional means are analytic witnesses and are not required computations of the reduction. Blocked use: ['Do not treat existence of the soundness witnesses as an efficient algorithm for extracting them.']
- **limit-6** (edge_case): Indexed vertices remain distinct even when their queries or vectors coincide. Blocked use: ['Do not merge equal vectors or change the uniform vertex distribution by deduplication.']
- **limit-7** (edge_case): A query can have no valid labels; its increment is then zero. Blocked use: ['Do not assume every query has an available label or select an unused slot.']
- **limit-8** (hypothesis): Compatibility requires agreement on overlaps and satisfaction of all constraints internal to the combined scopes. Blocked use: ['Do not replace compatibility with overlap agreement alone.', 'Do not require extension to a satisfying labeling of the entire source instance.']
- **limit-9** (hypothesis): Each decoded list uses only its own questions and weights; conditional kernels and list maps stay fixed when other data are later conditioned upon. Blocked use: ['Do not give a local decoder the opposite endpoint or sampled occurrence index.', 'Do not recompute conditional averages after fixing other realized batch inputs.']
- **limit-10** (edge_case): Conditional derivative identities hold almost everywhere, while the gradient hull and block bounds hold at every input. Blocked use: ['Do not assert the derivative identity at every exceptional weight tuple.']
- **limit-11** (hypothesis): The variance restriction retains the original product law for hidden seeds and batch weights after the frozen data are selected. Blocked use: ['Do not condition remaining draws on a success event or assume endpoint questions from the same seed are independent.']
- **limit-12** (approximation): Every graph edge test is exact; a floating-point replacement is not the stated construction. Blocked use: ['Do not certify an edge decision solely from approximate norm arithmetic.']
- **limit-13** (unknown): Average-case security, learning, distributed execution and deployed software contracts are ABSENT from the stated reduction. Blocked use: ['Do not infer cryptographic security, empirical heuristic performance or distributed guarantees from worst-case approximation hardness.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 102 (`lean/docs/102.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [The Unique Games Theorem](../../preprints/The-Unique-Games-Theorem-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [A Direct Proof of Optimal Max-Cut Hardness](../../preprints/A-Direct-Proof-of-Optimal-Max-Cut-Hardness-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [The Factor-Two Hardness Threshold for Vertex Cover](../../preprints/The-Factor-Two-Hardness-Threshold-for-Vertex-Cover-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Constant-factor hardness of Min-UnCut](../../preprints/Constant-factor-hardness-of-Min-UnCut-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Constant-factor hardness of directed feedback vertex set](../../preprints/Constant-factor-hardness-of-directed-feedback-vertex-set-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The Unique Games conjecture asks for hardness of distinguishing nearly satisfiable unique games from games of very small value. The formalized result gives, for every fixed $0<\varepsilon,\delta<1/2$, a deterministic polynomial-time reduction from binary 3SAT to nonempty unweighted simple bipartite unique games. Satisfiable inputs have value at least $1-\varepsilon$ and unsatisfiable inputs have value at most $\delta$. The alphabet is a fixed $\mathbb F_2^s$ depending only on the errors, and every constraint is a translation.
>
> The formalized result establishes hardness of approximating Max-Cut beyond the Goemans–Williamson constant $\alpha_{\mathrm{GW}}$. For every fixed $\alpha_{\mathrm{GW}}<\alpha\le1$, it gives a deterministic polynomial-time reduction from binary 3SAT to a strictly separated Max-Cut gap on finite simple unweighted graphs. The reduction includes the explicit positive integer scaling used in the gap statement.
>
> The formalized result gives the factor-two hardness threshold for Vertex Cover. For each integer $m\ge4$, a polynomial-time reduction from binary 3SAT produces simple unweighted graphs with cover density below $1/2+1/m$ on satisfiable inputs and above $1-1/m$ on unsatisfiable inputs. Consequently, any polynomial-time approximation with a fixed factor $1\le\alpha<2$ would give a polynomial-time decision algorithm for 3SAT. No assumption that $P\ne NP$ is built into the statement.
>
> Min-UnCut minimizes the number of edges left uncut by a bipartition. The formalization constructs a deterministic reduction from encoded $3$-SAT formulas to finite simple unweighted Min-UnCut instances with arbitrarily large fixed multiplicative gaps. For every integer $K\ge2$, satisfiable formulas give optimum at most the output threshold, while unsatisfiable formulas give optimum strictly greater than $K$ times that threshold. Runtime and output length are polynomial for each fixed $K$, establishing hardness for every fixed approximation factor greater than one.
>
> A directed feedback vertex set meets every directed cycle. The formalization proves hardness of approximating the minimum such set within any fixed constant factor. For every real factor $A\ge1$ and every language in NP, it constructs a polynomial-time gap reduction to unweighted directed graphs with the stated completeness and soundness separation. Thus a fixed-factor polynomial-time approximation would imply a polynomial-time algorithm for every NP language.

Comparator source `VertexCover.lean` (5120 bytes, sha256 `18604792a302754d02561f6901f4cc3eaf22aecae127f24f539d22371a513e36`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace UniqueGames.BinaryFormula

structure Literal where
  name : Nat
  positive : Bool
  deriving DecidableEq

def Literal.eval (literal : Literal) (assignment : Nat → Bool) : Bool :=
  if literal.positive then assignment literal.name else !(assignment literal.name)

abbrev Clause := Vector Literal 3

def Clause.eval (clause : Clause) (assignment : Nat → Bool) : Bool :=
  (clause[0].eval assignment || clause[1].eval assignment) || clause[2].eval assignment

structure Formula where
  clauses : List Clause

def Formula.Satisfiable (F : Formula) : Prop :=
  ∃ assignment : Nat → Bool, ∀ clause ∈ F.clauses, clause.eval assignment = true

end UniqueGames.BinaryFormula

namespace UniqueGames.BinaryEncoding

open BinaryFormula

def bitsValue : List Bool → Nat
  | [] => 0
  | b :: bits => Nat.bit b (bitsValue bits)

def frame : List Bool → List Bool
  | [] => [false]
  | b :: bits => true :: b :: frame bits

def parseFrame : List Bool → Option (List Bool × List Bool)
  | false :: rest => some ([], rest)
  | true :: b :: rest => do
      let (bits, trailing) ← parseFrame rest
      return (b :: bits, trailing)
  | _ => none

def nameBits (name : Nat) : List Bool := frame name.bits

def parseName (input : List Bool) : Option (Nat × List Bool) := do
  let (digits, rest) ← parseFrame input
  let name := bitsValue digits
  if digits = name.bits then some (name, rest) else none

def parseLiteral : List Bool → Option (Literal × List Bool)
  | sign :: input => do
      let (name, rest) ← parseName input
      return (⟨name, sign⟩, rest)
  | [] => none

def parseClause (input : List Bool) : Option (Clause × List Bool) := do
  let (a, input) ← parseLiteral input
  let (b, input) ← parseLiteral input
  let (c, rest) ← parseLiteral input
  return (#v[a, b, c], rest)

def parseClauses : Nat → List Bool → Option (List Clause × List Bool)
  | 0, _ => none
  | _fuel + 1, false :: rest => some ([], rest)
  | fuel + 1, true :: input => do
      let (clause, input) ← parseClause input
      let (clauses, rest) ← parseClauses fuel input
      return (clause :: clauses, rest)
  | _ + 1, [] => none

def decodeFormula (input : List Bool) : Option Formula := do
  let (clauses, rest) ← parseClauses (input.length + 1) input
  if rest = [] then some ⟨clauses⟩ else none

end UniqueGames.BinaryEncoding

namespace VertexCover

def ThreeSAT (input : List Bool) : Prop :=
  ∃ formula, UniqueGames.BinaryEncoding.decodeFormula input = some formula ∧
    formula.Satisfiable

structure ExplicitGraph where
  n : ℕ
  edges : List (Fin n × Fin n)
  increasing : ∀ e ∈ edges, e.1 < e.2
  nodup : edges.Nodup

namespace ExplicitGraph

def Cover (G : ExplicitGraph) (S : Finset (Fin G.n)) : Prop :=
  ∀ e ∈ G.edges, e.1 ∈ S ∨ e.2 ∈ S

instance (G : ExplicitGraph) (S : Finset (Fin G.n)) : Decidable (G.Cover S) :=
  inferInstanceAs (Decidable (∀ e ∈ G.edges, e.1 ∈ S ∨ e.2 ∈ S))

noncomputable def coverNumber (G : ExplicitGraph) : ℕ :=
  Nat.find (show ∃ k : ℕ, ∃ S : Finset (Fin G.n), G.Cover S ∧ S.card = k from
    ⟨G.n, Finset.univ, by simp [Cover], by simp⟩)

def bits (G : ExplicitGraph) : List Bool :=
  let names := [G.n] ++ List.range G.n ++ [G.edges.length] ++
    G.edges.flatMap (fun e => [e.1.val, e.2.val])
  names.flatMap UniqueGames.BinaryEncoding.nameBits

end ExplicitGraph

def FiniteAlphabet (M : Turing.FinTM2) : Prop := ∀ k, Finite (M.Γ k)

structure GapReduction (m : ℕ) where
  construct : List Bool → ExplicitGraph
  computation : Turing.TM2ComputableInPolyTime (id : List Bool → List Bool)
    ExplicitGraph.bits construct
  finiteAlphabet : FiniteAlphabet computation.tm
  completeness : ∀ input, ThreeSAT input →
    ((construct input).coverNumber : ℝ) <
      (1 / 2 + 1 / (m : ℝ)) * (construct input).n
  soundness : ∀ input, ¬ ThreeSAT input →
    (1 - 1 / (m : ℝ)) * (construct input).n <
      ((construct input).coverNumber : ℝ)

def natListBits (L : List ℕ) : List Bool :=
  (L.length :: L).flatMap UniqueGames.BinaryEncoding.nameBits

structure Approximation (α : ℝ) where
  run : ExplicitGraph → List ℕ
  computation : Turing.TM2ComputableInPolyTime ExplicitGraph.bits natListBits run
  finiteAlphabet : FiniteAlphabet computation.tm
  nodup : ∀ G, (run G).Nodup
  vertices : ∀ G, ∀ v ∈ run G, v < G.n
  covers : ∀ G, ∀ e ∈ G.edges, e.1.val ∈ run G ∨ e.2.val ∈ run G
  quality : ∀ G, ((run G).length : ℝ) ≤ α * (G.coverNumber : ℝ)

structure ThreeSATDecision where
  run : List Bool → Bool
  computation : Turing.TM2ComputableInPolyTime (id : List Bool → List Bool)
    (fun b : Bool => [b]) run
  finiteAlphabet : FiniteAlphabet computation.tm
  correct : ∀ input, run input = true ↔ ThreeSAT input

theorem explicit_cover_gap (m : ℕ) (hm : 4 ≤ m) : Nonempty (GapReduction m) := by
  sorry

theorem every_fixed_factor_below_two (α : ℝ) (hα : 1 ≤ α) (hα2 : α < 2)
    (algorithm : Approximation α) : Nonempty ThreeSATDecision := by
  sorry

end VertexCover

end OAI
```

The formalized result gives, for every fixed $0<\\varepsilon,\\delta<1/2$, a deterministic polynomial-time reduction from binary 3SAT to nonempty unweighted simple bipartite unique games. Satisfiable inputs have value at least $1-\\varepsilon$ and unsatisfiable inputs have value at most $\\delta$. The alphabet is a fixed $\\mathbb F_2^s$ depending only on the errors, and every constraint is a translation.\n\nThe formalized result establishes hardness of approximating Max-Cut beyond the Goemans–Williamson constant $\\alpha_{\\mathrm{GW}}$. For every fixed $\\alpha_{\\mathrm{GW}}<\\alpha\\le1$, it gives a deterministic polynomial-time reduction from binary 3SAT to a strictly separated Max-Cut gap on finite simple unweighted graphs. The reduction includes the explicit positive integer scaling used in the gap statement.\n\nThe formalized result gives the factor-two hardness threshold for Vertex Cover. For each integer $m\\ge4$, a polynomial-time reduction from binary 3SAT produces simple unweighted graphs with cover density below $1/2+1/m$ on satisfiable inputs and above $1-1/m$ on unsatisfiable inputs. Consequently, any polynomial-time approximation with a fixed factor $1\\le\\alpha<2$ would give a polynomial-time decision algorithm for 3SAT. No assumption that $P\\ne NP$ is built into the statement.\n\nMin-UnCut minimizes the number of edges left uncut by a bipartition. The formalization constructs a deterministic reduction from encoded $3$-SAT formulas to finite simple unweighted Min-UnCut instances with arbitrarily large fixed multiplicative gaps. For every integer $K\\ge2$, satisfiable formulas give optimum at most the output threshold, while unsatisfiable formulas give optimum strictly greater than $K$ times that threshold. Runtime and output length are polynomial for each fixed $K$, establishing hardness for every fixed approximation factor greater than one.\n\nA directed feedback vertex set meets every directed cycle. The formalization proves hardness of approximating the minimum such set within any fixed constant factor. For every real factor $A\\ge1$ and every language in NP, it constructs a polynomial-time gap reduction to unweighted directed graphs with the stated completeness and soundness separation. Thus a fixed-factor polynomial-time approximation would imply a polynomial-time algorithm for every NP language."

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that minimum Vertex Cover is NP-hard to approximate within every fixed factor below two, even on simple unweighted graphs.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

A vertex cover of a graph is a set of vertices meeting every edge. Its complement is an independent set, a set containing no two adjacent vertices. This elementary duality lets us express the hardness gap in terms of the densities of independent sets. For a finite simple undirected graph $G$, write $\tau(G)$ for the minimum size of a vertex cover and $n=|V(G)|$. An $\alpha$-approximation returns an actual cover of size at most $\alpha\tau(G)$. The endpoints of a maximal matching give a factor-$2$ approximation. We prove that every fixed improvement in this factor is NP-hard.

<a id="stmt-thm-gap"></a>
<div id="thm:gap" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Explicit cover gap). *For every fixed integer $m\geq4$, there is a deterministic polynomial-time reduction from $\mathrm{3SAT}$ to finite simple undirected unweighted graphs with the following guarantees: $$\begin{array}{ll}
\text{the formula is satisfiable}
 &\Longrightarrow\quad
 \tau(G)<\left(\dfrac12+\dfrac1m\right)|V(G)|,\\[6pt]
\text{the formula is unsatisfiable}
 &\Longrightarrow\quad
 \tau(G)>\left(1-\dfrac1m\right)|V(G)|.
\end{array}$$ The output lists all vertices and edges explicitly.*

</div>

<a id="stmt-thm-hardness"></a>
<div id="thm:hardness" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2**. *For every fixed real $\alpha$ with $1\leq\alpha<2$, approximating Vertex Cover within factor $\alpha$ is NP-hard. Thus, unless $\mathrm P=\mathrm{NP}$, no deterministic polynomial-time algorithm achieves factor $2-\varepsilon$ for any fixed $\varepsilon\in(0,1]$.*

</div>

The ratio of the two thresholds is $2-6/(m+2)$, which tends to $2$. The reduction is polynomial for each fixed $m$; its constants are allowed to depend on the desired approximation factor. We prove the approximation consequence, including an exact rational separating threshold, in [sec:conclusion](#sec:conclusion).

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Historical context and the role of the method

Vertex Cover appeared as <span class="smallcaps">Node Cover</span> among the problems Karp proved NP-complete in 1972 (Karp 1972) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Karp1972](#ref-Karp1972)]. Exact hardness still leaves room for approximation, and the elementary maximal-matching algorithm achieves factor two. Subsequent algorithms improved this ratio by an amount depending on the graph order. Karakostas obtained $2-\Theta(1/\sqrt{\log n})$ using semidefinite programming (Karakostas 2009) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Karakostas2009](#ref-Karakostas2009)]. This saving tends to zero as $n$ grows. The threshold addressed here concerns a fixed saving below two, so it is compatible with such algorithmic improvements.

The connection between proof checking and approximation hardness was developed by Feige, Goldwasser, Lovász, Safra, and Szegedy (Feige et al. 1996) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[FGLSS96](#ref-FGLSS96)]. Their graph encodes accepting local proof views, with consistency of the answers governing adjacency. The PCP breakthrough of 1992, developed by Arora and Safra and completed in constant-query form by Arora, Lund, Motwani, Sudan, and Szegedy (Arora and Safra 1998; Arora et al. 1998) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[AroraSafra98](#ref-AroraSafra98), [ALMSS98](#ref-ALMSS98)], yielded constant approximation gaps for problems including Vertex Cover. Raz’s parallel repetition theorem (Raz 1998) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Raz1998](#ref-Raz1998)] reduces the soundness of projection games to any desired fixed positive constant while preserving perfect completeness. Together these results supply the Label Cover promise stated precisely in [src:hardness](#src:hardness), the only external hardness input used here.

For Vertex Cover, Håstad established hardness for every fixed factor below $7/6$ (Håstad 2001, Theorem 8.1) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Hastad2001](#ref-Hastad2001)]. Dinur and Safra raised this to every fixed factor below $10\sqrt5-21$, approximately $1.36068$ (Dinur and Safra 2005, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[DinurSafra2005](#ref-DinurSafra2005)]. Their analysis uses biased set families and Boolean-function structure to control independent-set density. Khot’s Unique Games Conjecture (Khot 2002) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Khot02](#ref-Khot02)] proposed a stronger source of approximation gaps. Assuming this conjecture, Khot and Regev obtained hardness for every fixed factor below two (Khot and Regev 2008) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[KhotRegev2008](#ref-KhotRegev2008)]. Their proof uses a biased long code and an equivalent, stronger formulation of the source promise that supports short lists of candidate labels. Friedgut’s approximation theorem for Boolean functions of low average sensitivity (Friedgut 1998) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Friedgut1998](#ref-Friedgut1998)] supplies bounded sets of relevant coordinates in that analysis.

The 2017–2018 developments in the $2$-to-$2$ Games program gave an unconditional advance. The Grassmann-graph reduction of Khot, Minzer, and Safra (Khot, Minzer, and Safra 2025) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[KhotMinzerSafra2025](#ref-KhotMinzerSafra2025)] <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[DKKMS2025](#ref-DKKMS2025)], the work of Dinur, Khot, Kindler, Minzer, and Safra (Dinur et al. 2025), and the contribution of Barak, Kothari, and Steurer (Barak, Kothari, and Steurer 2019) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[BarakKothariSteurer2019](#ref-BarakKothariSteurer2019)] culminated in the expansion theorem of Khot, Minzer, and Safra (Khot, Minzer, and Safra 2023) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[KhotMinzerSafra2023](#ref-KhotMinzerSafra2023)]. This completed the Games Theorem with imperfect completeness and yielded unconditional Vertex Cover hardness for every fixed factor below $\sqrt2$. The resulting independent-set gap has completeness density close to $1-1/\sqrt2$ and arbitrarily small soundness density. The gap in [thm:gap](#thm:gap) instead has completeness density approaching $1/2$, which is what gives the factor-two threshold.

Our argument retains the objective of extracting short local label lists from a large independent set, but obtains the lists from Lipschitz derivatives. The central step preserves variance while restricting the information available to each list. We adapt the product-space orthogonal decomposition of the Efron–Stein method (Efron and Stein 1981, sec. 2) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[EfronStein1981](#ref-EfronStein1981)], combining it with a resampling estimate that controls the loss caused by this information restriction. A variance inequality on a product of intervals then supplies gradient energy, from which short candidate-label lists can be extracted without an alphabet-size factor. These ingredients are proved in full in [sec:restriction](#sec:restriction), [ana:private-section](#ana:private-section); the final decoding uses the ordinary Label Cover promise above.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The construction and the soundness mechanism

A Label Cover instance is a bipartite constraint system: every edge asks that a specified map send the label on its left endpoint to the label on its right endpoint. Its value is the largest fraction of constraints satisfied by one assignment of labels to all variables. Our source promise separates value one from arbitrarily small fixed value. The alphabets may grow as that fixed value decreases; our analytic estimates will not depend on their sizes. The exact source theorem is stated in [src:hardness](#src:hardness).

Imagine $d$ positions, with an independent source constraint assigned to each unordered pair. At a pair $j<k$, position $j$ receives the constraint’s left endpoint and position $k$ its right endpoint. A position’s *query* is its type together with these $d-1$ endpoint questions. It does not reveal constraint indices or opposite endpoints. A local label assigns symbols to the variables in one query and satisfies every source constraint internal to that scope. A compatible family chooses at most one label per query; its members agree on overlaps and satisfy every constraint internal to their combined scopes.

Give one vector coordinate to each query–label pair. A compatible family acts on a vector by summing the coordinates it selects; the largest absolute value of these sums defines a norm, $\left\|\cdot\right\|_*$. At each position, a vector of grid weights is supported on that position’s query block. A graph vertex specifies all pair constraints and all position weights. Let $S_v$ be the sum of its position vectors. For a positive threshold $t$, distinct vertices $v,w$ are adjacent precisely when $\left\|S_v+S_w\right\|_*\leq2t$.

A satisfying source assignment restricts to compatible labels for every query. Its linear functional exceeds $t$ on an independent set: on the sum of two such vertices it exceeds $2t$. A tail estimate for a sum of symmetric weights shows that this set occupies nearly half the graph. [graph:section](#graph:section) gives the construction, its deterministic implementation, and this completeness argument.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
###### Why endpoint information matters.

For soundness, we will extract short lists of local labels from a large independent set. Each list must depend only on its position’s own query and weights. To see why, fix a pair of positions and all data except their shared source constraint. Each of the two lists then varies only with its own endpoint of that constraint. Choosing one rank in each list defines a labeling of all source variables, so source soundness bounds the probability that these two labels are compatible. This argument fails if either list also sees the opposite endpoint or the occurrence index. The main task is therefore to obtain both enough compatibility among the lists and this restriction on their information.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
###### Retaining variance in own-question means.

Discard from a large independent set its at most one vertex with vector norm at most $t$. The remaining vectors are separated from the negatives of one another. The half-difference of their distance functions gives one fixed odd Lipschitz function $F$. Replace the grid weights by independent continuous uniform weights on cubes; rounding and the Lipschitz bound control the error. On fresh random sums, $F$ then has variance at least a fixed multiple of $d$, as proved in [ana:function](#ana:function).

Choose a small batch $J$ of $h$ positions, freeze the weights outside it and the constraints on pairs not contained in it, and average $F$ conditional only on one remaining position’s own endpoint questions and weights. Such averaging might erase the variance. [res:private-variance](#res:private-variance) shows that a suitable restriction retains total variance at least a fixed multiple of $h$ in these own-question means. The proof decomposes over the independent weights and pair seeds. Its crucial estimate resamples every seed incident to one position while preserving the questions received at all other positions. Only the first position’s query can then change. This bounds the energy lost when the conditional means forget other positions’ questions, independently of the label alphabets.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
###### From variance to compatible lists.

For fixed batch queries, differentiate $F$ composed with the sum of the position vectors. The full gradient lies in the convex hull of signed incidence vectors of compatible label selections (). It carries a compatible representation, but can depend on all batch inputs. Conversely, the derivatives of the own-question means have the required information restriction, but need not together lie in that same hull.

The cube variance bound in [ana:cube](#ana:cube) gives enough squared energy in the own-question gradients. Each block has $\ell^1$ norm at most one, so thresholding its entries produces a short list without an alphabet-size factor. Conditional Jensen then transfers the retained list mass back to the single full gradient before its compatible-incidence bound is used. If $Z$ is the largest number of lists hit by one compatible selection, this gives $\mathbb EZ\geq2$ (). The pair-decoding argument above, summed over pairs of positions, gives $\mathbb EZ<3/2$ under source soundness (), a contradiction.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
###### Organization.

[sec:source](#sec:source) states the source promise and introduces the construction parameters. [graph:section](#graph:section) constructs the graph and proves completeness; [ana:section](#ana:section) converts an independent set into the analytic input. The restriction, gradient, and decoding arguments occupy [sec:restriction](#sec:restriction), [ana:private-section](#ana:private-section), [dec:section](#dec:section), respectively. [sec:conclusion](#sec:conclusion) verifies a joint choice of all parameters and turns the density gap into the approximation threshold.

The construction enumerates all its finite seeds and grid weights. Random variables describe the uniform distribution on this output and appear only in its analysis. Neither the independent set, the function $F$, nor the conditional means need to be computed by the reduction.

<a id="sec:source"></a>
### The source problem and construction parameters

Write $[k]=\{1,\ldots,k\}$. A *Label Cover instance* $\Phi$ consists of two disjoint finite variable sets $U,V$, two nonempty finite alphabets $\Sigma_U,\Sigma_V$, and an explicit nonempty list of $M$ constraint occurrences. Occurrence $c\in[M]$ has endpoints $x_c\in U$, $y_c\in V$, and a total map $\pi_c:\Sigma_U\to\Sigma_V$. A labeling $A$ assigns each variable a symbol from its side’s alphabet. It satisfies occurrence $c$ when $$\pi_c(A(x_c))=A(y_c).$$ The value $\mathop{\mathrm{val}}(\Phi)$ is the maximum fraction of satisfied occurrences. List entries count with their multiplicity. Endpoints and map tables are given explicitly in binary; unused variable declarations can be removed. We require no regularity or expansion condition on the constraint graph.

<a id="stmt-src-hardness"></a>
<div id="src:hardness" class="theorem">

**Theorem 3** (Perfect-completeness Label Cover hardness). *For every fixed rational $\sigma\in(0,1)$, there are nonempty constant alphabets and a deterministic polynomial-time reduction from $\mathrm{3SAT}$ to instances of the form above such that satisfiable formulas give $\mathop{\mathrm{val}}(\Phi)=1$, and unsatisfiable formulas give $\mathop{\mathrm{val}}(\Phi)\leq\sigma$.*

</div>

This is the standard consequence of the probabilistically checkable proof (PCP) theorem (Arora and Safra 1998; Arora et al. 1998) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[AroraSafra98](#ref-AroraSafra98), [ALMSS98](#ref-ALMSS98)] and parallel repetition (Raz 1998) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Raz1998](#ref-Raz1998)]. A quantitative formulation appears in the full version of Dinur and Steurer (Dinur and Steurer 2014, Theorem 6.2) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[DinurSteurer2014](#ref-DinurSteurer2014)]. The precise unweighted, total-projection formulation is recorded in (Dinur et al. 2025, Definitions 1.1–1.2 and Theorem 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[DKKMS2025](#ref-DKKMS2025)]. There the constraints are even $D$-to-one for a suitable constant $D$, a property we do not use. The constants, including the alphabets and $D$, may depend on $\sigma$. This theorem is the only external hardness input to our proof.

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### Parameters for the construction

<a id="src:dimension-bounds"></a>
Fix the requested gap integer $m\geq4$. We first construct and analyze graphs for arbitrary fixed source alphabets and an even square integer $d$ satisfying $$d\geq4,\qquad \sqrt d>4m.
 \label{src:dimension-bounds}$$ The number $d$ will count the positions in the construction. The soundness argument will impose further lower bounds on $d$ and determine the required source soundness. In [sec:conclusion](#sec:conclusion) we choose all these constants as functions of $m$, before invoking [src:hardness](#src:hardness) to obtain the alphabets.

For the graph construction put <a id="src:construction-parameters"></a>$$a=\frac1{4m},\qquad p=2d+1,\qquad t=a\sqrt d.
 \label{src:construction-parameters}$$ The graph will use a grid with $p$ points and an adjacency threshold $2t$. These choices give $$\frac dp<\frac12<\frac t2,\qquad p>2m.$$ Both $p$ and $\sqrt d$ are integers, so $t$ is rational. For a source instance over fixed alphabets, set $$q=\max\{|\Sigma_U|,|\Sigma_V|\},\qquad r=q^{d-1}.$$ A query will involve at most $d-1$ variables, and $r$ bounds its number of local labels. For fixed $m,d$ and alphabets, every parameter introduced here is constant with respect to the source input size.

<a id="graph:section"></a>
### The graph and completeness

Fix a source instance and the construction parameters from [sec:source](#sec:source). Each vertex will specify source constraints on the pairs of $d$ positions and weights on the labels available at each position. We first define when local labels fit together and use their coordinate sums to define a norm. A satisfying source labeling will then provide a linear functional whose values above $t$ select an independent set.

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### Queries and compatible labels

Let $\mathcal E=\binom{[d]}2$ be the set of unordered pairs of distinct positions. A query of type $j\in[d]$ has the form $$i=\bigl(j,(q_{jk})_{k\in[d]\setminus\{j\}}\bigr),
 \qquad
 q_{jk}\in
 \begin{cases}
  V,&k<j,\\
  U,&k>j.
 \end{cases}$$ The tuple is ordered by increasing $k$. Let $\mathcal Q$ be the set of all such queries of all types, and let $P(i)\subseteq U\cup V$ be the set of distinct variables appearing in $i$. The side of each variable is part of its identity, since $U$ and $V$ are disjoint. Repeated entries in the tuple contribute only one variable to $P(i)$; thus $|P(i)|\leq d-1$.

A label $\lambda$ for $i$ assigns a symbol from the appropriate alphabet to each variable of $P(i)$ and satisfies every source constraint occurrence whose two endpoints belong to $P(i)$. Write $\Lambda_i$ for the set of these labels. This set may be empty, for example when constraints internal to the scope are inconsistent. There are at most $q^{|P(i)|}\leq r$ labels. Fix orders on variables and alphabet symbols, and list the valid assignments in lexicographic order to define an injection $$\operatorname{slot}_i:\Lambda_i\longrightarrow[r].$$ This is the empty map when $\Lambda_i=\varnothing$. Slots outside its image will play no role in an increment.

Define the finite coordinate set $$\mathcal P=\{(i,\lambda):i\in\mathcal Q,\ \lambda\in\Lambda_i\}.$$ A *compatible selection* is a subset $I\subseteq\mathcal P$ with at most one label per query, such that the selected labels agree on overlaps and their induced labeling satisfies every source constraint whose two endpoints lie in $$P(I)=\bigcup_{(i,\lambda)\in I}P(i).$$ Let $\mathcal I$ be the family of compatible selections, including the empty selection. Agreement on overlaps alone is not enough: constraints joining variables from different selected scopes must also be satisfied. Thus labels on any two selected queries satisfy every source constraint between their scopes. Compatibility imposes no condition on a constraint with an endpoint outside $P(I)$; the induced labeling need not extend to a satisfying labeling of the entire instance.

<a id="graph:norm-definition"></a>
For $z\in\mathbb R^{\mathcal P}$, define $$\label{graph:norm-definition}
 \left\|z\right\|_*=\max_{I\in\mathcal I}
       \left|\sum_{(i,\lambda)\in I}z_{i,\lambda}\right|.$$ For a query $i$ and a vector $s\in\mathbb R^r$, let $z(i,s)$ be supported on its query block, with $$z(i,s)_{i,\lambda}=s_{\operatorname{slot}_i(\lambda)}
 \quad\text{for }\lambda\in\Lambda_i.$$ All coordinates in other query blocks are zero. In particular, $z(i,s)=0$ for a query with no valid label.

<a id="stmt-graph-norm"></a>
<div id="graph:norm" class="lemma">

<a id="graph:increment"></a>
**Lemma 4** (Compatibility norm). *The family $\mathcal I$ is hereditary and contains every coordinate singleton. Formula <a href="#graph:norm-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="graph:norm-definition">[graph:norm-definition]</a> defines a norm. For each fixed query $i$, the increment is linear in $s$ and obeys $$\label{graph:increment}
 \left\|z(i,s)\right\|_*\leq\|s\|_\infty.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-graph-norm"><summary>Proof of stmt-graph-norm</summary>

*Proof.* Removing labels preserves agreement and can only shrink the scope union. Every constraint internal to the smaller union was therefore satisfied before removal. This proves heredity. Every singleton belongs to $\mathcal I$ by the definition of a valid query label. The maximum of absolute linear forms is absolutely homogeneous and satisfies the triangle inequality; the singleton forms ensure that it vanishes only at zero. If the coordinate set is empty, this is the unique norm on the zero-dimensional space. Finally, a compatible selection contains at most one coordinate of the $i$-block, proving <a href="#graph:increment" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="graph:increment">[graph:increment]</a>. Linearity follows from the coordinate formula. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-13"></a>
#### Vertices and edges

A seed tuple is an indexed family $\mathbf c=(c_e)_{e\in\mathcal E}\in[M]^{\mathcal E}$. For $e=\{j,k\}$ with $j<k$, seed $c_e$ gives endpoint $x_{c_e}$ to position $j$ and endpoint $y_{c_e}$ to position $k$. It thereby specifies queries $i_j(\mathbf c)$ through $$q_{jk}=x_{c_{\{j,k\}}},\qquad
 q_{kj}=y_{c_{\{j,k\}}}\qquad(j<k).$$ A query retains its type and its ordered endpoint questions. It does not retain the sampled occurrence indices, the sampled projection maps, or the opposite endpoint in any pair. Different seed tuples can give the same queries. Queries of different position types are nevertheless distinct, even if some endpoint values coincide. The source instance used to define labels and slots is fixed; the seed-dependent data in a query are only its endpoint questions.

<a id="graph:edge"></a>
<a id="graph:size"></a>
Use the symmetric grid <a id="graph:grid"></a>$$\label{graph:grid}
 \mathcal T=\left\{\frac{2u}{p}:u=-d,-d+1,\ldots,d\right\},
 \qquad p=2d+1.$$ These are the midpoints of the $p$ equal consecutive subintervals of $[-1,1]$. The vertex set consists of all pairs $$v=(\mathbf c,\mathbf s),\qquad
 \mathbf c\in[M]^{\mathcal E},\quad
 \mathbf s=(s_1,\ldots,s_d)\in(\mathcal T^r)^d.$$ Each indexed pair is one vertex, including when another pair produces the same queries or the same vector <a id="graph:sum-vector"></a>$$\label{graph:sum-vector}
 S_v=\sum_{j=1}^d z(i_j(\mathbf c),s_j).$$ For two distinct vertices, declare $$\label{graph:edge}
 \{v,w\}\in E(G)
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \left\|S_v+S_w\right\|_*\leq2t.$$ This defines a finite simple undirected unweighted graph. Its order is $$\label{graph:size}
 n=|V(G)|=M^{\binom d2}p^{rd}\geq p>2m,$$ where the inequalities use $M,r\geq1$ and [src:dimension-bounds](#src:dimension-bounds), [src:construction-parameters](#src:construction-parameters). Choosing a uniform vertex is exactly choosing all seeds independently and uniformly from $[M]$ and all weight entries independently and uniformly from $\mathcal T$. There is no identification of equal vectors in this probability law.

<a id="stmt-graph-local-norm"></a>
<div id="graph:local-norm" class="lemma">

<a id="graph:local-formula"></a>
**Lemma 5** (Local evaluation of the norm). *Let $\mathcal B\subseteq\mathcal Q$ be a set of query blocks supporting $z\in\mathbb R^{\mathcal P}$. Then $$\label{graph:local-formula}
 \left\|z\right\|_*=\max_{\substack{I\in\mathcal I:\\
                          (i,\lambda)\in I\ \Longrightarrow\ i\in\mathcal B}}
       \left|\sum_{(i,\lambda)\in I}z_{i,\lambda}\right|.$$ For $z=S_v+S_w$, this maximum can be evaluated by at most $(1+r)^{2d}$ choices, each checked by a scan of the source constraints.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-graph-local-norm"><summary>Proof of stmt-graph-local-norm</summary>

*Proof.* Intersect any compatible selection with the blocks in $\mathcal B$. Heredity preserves compatibility, and the sum is unchanged because all other blocks of $z$ vanish. Conversely, each selection on the right of <a href="#graph:local-formula" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="graph:local-formula">[graph:local-formula]</a> is a selection in the full maximum. This proves equality.

For a pair of vertices there are at most $2d$ distinct query blocks. First aggregate the coordinate contributions of all occurrences of each repeated query. For each distinct query $i$, enumerate the at most $q^{d-1}\leq r$ assignments on its scope, and keep exactly those satisfying every source constraint internal to that scope. This computes $\Lambda_i$ and its slots. Next choose either no label or one of these labels in each block. There are at most $(1+r)^{2d}$ choices. For each choice, test agreement on repeated variables and scan the source list to test every constraint internal to the union of the chosen scopes. This union has at most $2d(d-1)$ variables. The surviving choices are precisely the selections on the right of <a href="#graph:local-formula" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="graph:local-formula">[graph:local-formula]</a>, and their absolute coordinate sums give the required maximum. Neither the labels nor the compatibility test requires satisfying any constraint outside the indicated scope union. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-graph-polynomial"></a>
<div id="graph:polynomial" class="proposition">

**Proposition 6** (Deterministic polynomial construction). *For a fixed integer $m\geq4$, a fixed even square $d$ satisfying [src:dimension-bounds](#src:dimension-bounds), and fixed source alphabets, the graph $G$ can be output explicitly in deterministic time polynomial in the source bit length. Every edge test is exact.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-graph-polynomial"><summary>Proof of stmt-graph-polynomial</summary>

*Proof.* Let $L$ be the source bit length. The fixed integers $m,d$ determine $p$ and the rational number $t$ by [src:construction-parameters](#src:construction-parameters). The fixed alphabets determine $q$ and $r=q^{d-1}$. All these quantities are therefore computable constants independent of $L$. Since the constraint list is explicit, $M=O(L)$, and <a href="#graph:size" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="graph:size">[graph:size]</a> gives $n=O(L^{\binom d2})$. A vertex description contains a fixed number of source indices and grid entries. Thus all vertices can be enumerated and assigned identifiers of $O(1+\log n)$ bits.

Apply [graph:local-norm](#graph:local-norm) to each unordered pair of distinct vertices. All label and selection enumerations have a fixed number of choices. Variable comparisons, sorting, constraint scans, and projection-map evaluations take polynomial bit time in $L$. The arithmetic of the edge test uses only rational grid entries. In fact, every coordinate of $S_v+S_w$ has denominator $p$, after summing all repeated-block contributions. The local maximum is therefore $\zeta/p$ for a nonnegative integer $\zeta$. By <a href="#graph:increment" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="graph:increment">[graph:increment]</a> and the triangle inequality, $\zeta/p\leq2d$, so its numerator and denominator have bit lengths bounded solely in terms of the fixed parameters. Since $t=\sqrt d/(4m)$ and $\sqrt d$ is an integer, the edge test is precisely $$2m\zeta\leq p\sqrt d.$$ Both sides are integers, so the test involves no approximation of a real number.

Checking all pairs and writing every qualifying edge takes $$O\bigl((1+n^2)\operatorname{poly}(L+\log n)\bigr)$$ bit operations, with constants depending only on the fixed parameters and alphabets. Formula <a href="#graph:size" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="graph:size">[graph:size]</a> also computes $n$ by integer arithmetic with $O(1+\log n)$ output bits. Thus the construction has polynomial bit complexity, including its explicit vertex and edge lists. It runs on every source instance with these alphabets, regardless of whether the gap promise holds. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-14"></a>
#### Completeness

A satisfying source assignment will give a linear functional whose value exceeds $t$ on an independent set. To show that this set occupies nearly half the vertices, we first bound the corresponding tail of a sum of independent uniform grid variables.

<a id="stmt-graph-anticoncentration"></a>
<div id="graph:anticoncentration" class="lemma">

<a id="graph:tail"></a>
**Lemma 7** (Midpoint-grid tail). *If $T_1,\ldots,T_d$ are independent uniform elements of $\mathcal T$, then $$\label{graph:tail}
 \Pr\left[\sum_{j=1}^dT_j>t\right]
 >\frac12-\sqrt2\left(a+\frac1{\sqrt d}\right)
 >\frac12-\frac1m.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-graph-anticoncentration"><summary>Proof of stmt-graph-anticoncentration</summary>
